Boats arriving from Tangyng pay full inward dues if discharging at Shasi town, local trade dues if discharging near the West station; but in theShipping table they are all entered as local traders. In the same way boats from Shasi town to Tangyang pay full outward dues, but are classed among local clearances. Boats arriving from Tangyang for other ports pay full outward dues. They are entered in the Shipping table first as local entries, then as interport clearances. Boats arriving from other ports for Tangyang have usually no dues to pay, but in the Shipping table they are entered, firstly, as interport entries and, secondly, as local clearances. 11.-IMPORTS. 1*. PART III. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended June 30, 1910.
278 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 485. TIENTSIN DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN POSITION OF LIGHT-VESSEL &quot;TAKU.&quot; NOTICE is hereby given that the light-vessel Taku, has been shifted, and now lies in 24 feet of water at low water of spring tides in the following position:- Fort Light bearing N. 730 W., distant 8 miles. Vessels are not to anchor to the northward of the line joining the light-vessel and the Entrance Buoy. SHANGHAI, 12th April 19o. No. 486. CANTON DISTRICT. UNCHARTED ROOK IN HEUNGCHOW BAY. NOTICE is hereby given that in Heungehow Bay an uncharted rock, covered about 3 feet at low water of spring tides, is situated with White Rock bearing S. 87* E. (magnetic), distant 7.5 cables. Heungehow Bay, unnamed on the charts, is the bay 7 miles to the northward of Macao. SHANGHAI, 18th MaU 10io.
1910.] NOTIFICATIONS. 279 No. 487. TIENTSIN DISTRICT. ALTERATION IN AIDS TO NAVIGATION IN THE APPROACH TO THE PEIHO. NOTICE is hereby given of the following alterations in the aids to navigation in the approach to the Peiho:- The Fairway Buoy, South Buoy, North Bank Spit Buoy, and Inner Buoy are discontinued. The Bar Light-boat has been shifted to mark the northern side of the channel at the western entrance to Ferguson Channel. SHANGHIo, 19th May 19o. No. 488. CANTON DISTRICT. UNCHARTED ROCK IN HEUNGCHOW BAY. REFERRING to Notice, to Mariners NO. 486, notice is hereby given that the rock in Heungehow Bay, referred to therein, dries 3 feet at low water of spring tides. SHANGHAI, 2nd June igio.
280 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [APRIL-JUNE, 1910.] No. 489. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. CONFUCIUS CHANNEL. ExTENsioN OF SOUTH-EAST SPIT; ALTERATIONS IN BUOYAGE. NoTicE is hereby given that the South-east Spit has extended about 3 miles to the south-eastward of its position as shown on the latest charts, this extension having from 4 to 5 fathoms on it at low water of spring tides. The South-east Spit Gas-lighted Buoy has been shifted to mark the 5 fathom contour off the right bank of the river abreast of the end. of the spit and has been changed in colour to black. It lies in 29 feet of water at low water of spring tides, with Liu Creek Joss House bearing S. 80 E. (magnetic), distant 2.9 miles, and marks the port side of the channel for vessels bound up river. A red 6-foot buoy, having a black triangular cage, to be known as the Dove&#39;s Nest Lower Buoy, has been placed in the former position of the South-east Spit Gas-lighted Buoy. SHANGHAI, 3rd June 1910. No. 490. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. NORTH CHANNEL ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. NOToE is hereby given that the Drinkwater Point Gas-lighted Buoy has been altered in colour to red and now lies in 18 feet of water at low water of spring tides, with Shaweishan Lighthouse bearing N. 850 E., distant 12.2 miles. It now marks the starboard side of the channel for vessels bound in. SHANGHAI, 14th June iOio.
